#7211. Reddit dangles seasonal ad credits but advertisers still want better proof it works all year round

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

Reddit is offering advertisers extra incentives to spend in Q4 — the stretch of the year when budgets are biggest and competition for them is fiercest.

The social news website is offering new advertisers credits on new campaigns that go live before November 20: in the U.K., they get £500 for spending £1,000, or £2,000 for spending £2,000, according to an ad exec who recently spoke with their Reddit rep and shared an email confirming the incentives and deadline. In the U.S., the platform is also offering new advertisers $1,500 in ad credits when they spend $1,500 on the platform, per ads seen by Digiday.

After reviewing the incentives that Digiday had seen, Accuracast’s group CEO Farhad Divecha said that incentives like this “do work to reignite dormant clients’ interest to retry the platform.” However, following the decline in Google Search referrals, the general sentiment among his team’s clients is that Reddit isn’t as attractive without ChatGPT and Google sending it all that traffic.

The dip Divecha is referring to is the one Reddit disclosed on its earnings call last month when CEO Steve Huffman said October search referrals were down by more than one million a week on average, in part because of Google’s AI search features. To be clear, the credits aren’t tied to the slump. Q4 incentives are a fixture of the platform ad calendar and Reddit’s deadline for them is timed to get new campaigns running before Black Friday. That said, the extra spend could still help offset some of the hit from search, at least for a quarter.

Because incentives only go so far. Keeping advertisers around once the credits run out takes an ads platform that does what marketers need it to do, from measurement to targeting to self-serve tools. Reddit is still building toward all of that.

“Reddit is making big strides to compete with their competitor set within social with new capabilities, features across targeting, bidding and measurement but ultimately still comes in last place,” said independent ad tech consultant Jonathan D’Souza Rauto. “Advertisers do like to test the waters with it but it doesn’t necessarily translate in always-on budgets.”

The incentives come at the end of a year in which the rate of Reddit’s ad revenue growth has slowed down over three consecutive quarters. In Q1 2026, Reddit recorded $625 million in ad revenue, up 74% year over year, per the company’s earnings reports. By Q2, ad revenue had increased to $762 million, the rate of growth had slipped to 64% on the same period a year ago. By Q3 2026, the platform announced $847 million in ad revenue, with growth falling again to 54% versus the same period a year ago, per its latest earnings.

“The slowdown in ad revenue growth and referral traffic may be occurring at the same time, but that doesn’t necessarily mean one is driving the other,” said Jessica Dervyn, senior principal analyst in Gartner’s Marketing Practice.

Reddit, however, points to its continued revenue growth and advertiser demand as evidence that the platform remains an attractive proposition for marketers.

“The numbers speak for themselves: we’ve grown ad revenue by more than 50% year over year for nine straight quarters, well ahead of most peers over the same timeframe,” said Natalie Maguire, director of global B2B communications at Reddit in an emailed statement to Digiday. “We continue to see strong demand from advertisers as they increasingly turn to Reddit to drive results across the funnel. Our ad incentive program has stayed consistent over the last year and we have not increased our incentives amount. It’s an ongoing part of our business and a standard practice across the industry.”

It’s a thought not lost on some advertisers.

Take James Mortimer at Roast, for example. He said that his team finds the platform “really valuable” for advertisers because the users are so engaged.

“There’s quite a lot of people on there [Reddit] you can’t reach on Meta,” he said. Mortimer acknowledged that Reddit isn’t for every advertiser, but said it was “definitely valuable for the right advertisers that can work on there.”

However, audience engagement alone isn’t necessarily enough to convince advertisers to shift more of their budgets to Reddit. Measurement remains an important part of the equation.

“That’s something where Meta is really good now, so it’s harder to make the case for a client who is spending on Meta already to then take budget and put it into another channel [like Reddit], which might not be as effective,” said Mortimer.

New Gen’s strategy director Mark Bellamy had a similar take, saying that search referrals are part of the story but not the main thing holding back ad spend on Reddit. The bigger picture, he said, is the ad product.

“Reddit is still a test-and-learn platform for us, not somewhere we’d confidently put full-funnel budget,” he said. “You can get good CPMs and decent awareness. What we haven’t seen enough of is proof that you can build an ecosystem there that drives quality traffic and nurtures it. Until we do, we’ll be cautious about recommending significant media spend.”

That puts the onus on Reddit to demonstrate that advertisers can get meaningful returns from the platform beyond the initial incentive. While promotional offers can bring new advertisers through the door, converting those trials into long-term investment is a different challenge entirely.

#7207. The case for and against AI token capping

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

When one of the world’s most valuable companies starts to get serious about AI waste, it’s time to pay attention. Apple is placing a hard cap of $200 on its engineers’ daily AI token use, and may reduce non-engineers’ token allowance to $25, Bloomberg reported last week (Nov. 4).

A handful of ad agencies have already embraced token capping this year — using AI software, or a bespoke tool, to prevent users from consuming more than a set amount of AI compute as the costs of AI transformation become clearer. Apple’s policy change indicates that more companies could follow that path, but there are tradeoffs involved in this strict approach to costs.

Agencies considering capping token usage must weigh up the risk they could douse their employees’ creativity by limiting their access to the tech.

AI costs — from tokens to licensing and subscriptions — are likely as cheap as they’ll ever be. While manageable today, they’re kept low because of the enormous capital expenditures of companies like Meta, OpenAI and Anthropic. Eventually, those firms will want a return on those investments, meaning today’s prices might not be stable for long.

With that in mind, marketing agencies are building the infrastructure of AI accountability today, rather than wait for a crisis. Performance agency Roast, for example, has capped the amount of AI tokens its staffers can use since March.

“It’s up to the individual to use those tokens responsibly, and I think the benefit of that is then it’s making people think about what use cases they use tokens for,” John Campbell, head of innovation and AI at Roast, told Digiday.

The company allots a set amount of tokens to each employee within its Claude subscription, Campbell said. Higher individual caps can be provided at particularly busy times. He noted that the limit had stopped some users from relying on the tech for repetitive tasks that might be better delegated to non-AI software tools.

Video and image production generally represents the most token-intensive activity for creative agencies; media agencies experimenting with agentic tools for planning and buying must also grapple with runaway operating costs.

U.S. media agency PMG places a $50 daily limit on staff AI usage through its Alli4U portal. Staff that hit the cap more than once can be assigned a larger token budget, or receive advice on using different AI models, Dillon Larberg, consulting and strategy director, told Digiday in September. “That provides us a human in the loop moment to meet with teams or meet with individuals,” he said.

While agencies might still want to allow staff the flexibility to experiment and play with AI tools they can use tiered capping systems to provide teams with enough leeway to pilot something new, without burning up the budget.

“If you say you have a limit of $30, $25, $10 a day, you’ll find people become very creative in how they get this stuff done based on that constraint,” said Jonathan Whiteside, global executive vp at Dept, which caps team AI usage rather than individual costs.

“Constraints are sometimes useful for us to find new ways of working,” Whiteside added. “People start to think much more carefully about, ‘Well, do I actually need to use AI to do this?’”

AI costs, including the price of tokens, won’t stay low forever, so agencies should make hay while the sun’s shining. Intentionally cutting back before compute costs rise would be like abstaining from MoviePass or WeWork in the glorious days of zero interest rates. Agencies can chew through tokens while they develop the best uses for AI tech now, while it’s cheap to do so.

Some agencies use soft AI caps, rather than a hard limit. “When someone maxes out their spend for that tier, they show their work and their output to their department head,” explained Daniel Gilbert, CEO of Brainlabs, in September. He described the company’s approach as one that embraced “checkpoints” rather than caps. “The department head goes through the work with them, and if it’s good work, then they push them into the next tier.”

Such an approach preserves staff autonomy while keeping a lid on AI spending for agencies that want to to let a hundred flowers, or AI agents, bloom.

There are also means of nudging staff into cost-conscious AI decisions, without placing concrete limits on their usage. Gateway systems, or “gating,” refers to a tool which reroutes a user from a token-intensive AI model to a less powerful (and cheaper) model, if it’s better suited to a user’s project. If they’re using ChatGPT to draft their LinkedIn posts, they probably don’t need Astra, its latest and most powerful model.

Whiteside said that model selection is “a big factor” in keeping costs down, but that gating systems or staff training can provide a more appropriate guardrail than an individual token allowance.

As the real costs of AI-enabled marketing no services become clearer, expect more agencies and brands to consider whether hard caps or flexibility are most important.

#7203. OpenAI’s new business agents give brands a free way into the conversation it’s also selling as an ad

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

The sponsored link had a good run. Its successor might talk back.

OpenAI certainly thinks so. Businesses can now claim a profile in ChatGPT through a new Business Hub, get verified and feed in product and company details. In the U.S. (for now), e-commerce and online services businesses can go a step further and publish a business agent – a separately labelled chat that speaks for the brand once a user wants to dig into a product. OpenAI’s vp of monetization Benji Shomair declined to share a timeline for further rollout.

He did, however, clarify that the business agent is the free, organic way into a format Digiday reported on in September, when OpenAI started testing it as a paid ad.

“Think of the business agent as the organic experience,” Shomair said during a press briefing. “The sponsored agent is the paid experience, but the agent that sits behind it should be the same.”

So, OpenAI has built two routes into the same conversation for brands, and only one of them costs money. One through ChatGPT’s organic recommendations, and the other via paid placements.

Shomair said early testing of sponsored agents had shown “promising results,” particularly in the depth of conversations between consumers and businesses.

“When a consumer chooses to engage more deeply with our business, there’s a high level of intent there,” he said.

Shomair described typical consumer use cases as working through product choices, weighing up size, colour and price before exploring related purchases. For businesses, that could result in turning an initial product enquiry into a bigger basket, and ultimately a deeper customer relationship.

When the shopper is ready to pay, the agent waves them off to the brand’s own site, because “it’s up to the business on how they want to close the transaction,” said Shomair.

Whatever got said before that point stays with OpenAI. Brands hoping to read back their agents’ transcripts get themes instead.

“The only insight provided today are the ones we sort of shared there initially, which are the themes of the conversations where you’re appearing,” Shomair said.

OpenAI keeps hold of the agent itself too. It’s built from the instructions and product feed a business supplies with no room for an in-house version. “We aren’t allowing folks to bring their own agents,” said Shomair.

The rest of the Business Hub sits under Insights, the the analytics dashboard-type product that Digiday reported on this week — the beta shows mentions, views and website clicks, plus discovery topics.

“Those are common themes in conversations where ChatGPT cites the business’s site,” an OpenAI spokesperson confirmed to Digiday. “Businesses don’t see individual prompts or chats. Share of voice isn’t part of this beta.”

How mentions are tracked depends on the type of business. For local businesses, a mention counts when ChatGPT shows a result for that location. For an online business, it counts when ChatGPT references the business’s website.

OpenAI declined to say how long mentions are tracked. An ad exec with access to Insights within the Business Hub did share that the chart within the feature shows everything within the past seven or 28 days.

Accuracast’s group CEO Farhad Divecha said that his team has been reliant on tools like Peec,.AI, Otterly.AI or Semrush to guesstimate brand visibility on ChatGPT as well as other AI platforms. But he noted that they are all using approximations from Google search data, third-party traffic estimators and their own data modeling tools, and none have access to original data from the source.

“Now that OpenAI is making their own mentions data available, we’re very interested to see how far off these tools were from the actual data from ChatGPT, and whether these tools will be able to adapt and incorporate the OpenAI data,” Divecha said. “If their estimation data was wildly erroneous, that will make us rethink paying for those tools.”

The implications extend beyond the accuracy of individual tools. OpenAI’s decision to share its own data could also shift the balance of power in the growing AI visibility industry. As Sonata Insights’ founder and chief analyst Debra Aho Williamson pointed out, the entire AI visibility (AEO/GEO) industry is built on third-party research (and some guesswork) about what helps a brand show up in an answer.

“The fact that OpenAI will now share insights itself will take some of the power away from the third-party companies,” she said. “But OpenAI isn’t going to share everything it knows, and ChatGPT is only one of several AI platforms, so demand for what the AI visibility companies offer will remain strong.”

Business Hub and agents are free for now but Shomair didn’t rule out a paid model in the future.

“In terms of where they develop in the future, I think it’ll depend on the directions that they go,” he said.

What none of it buys is a leg-up in ChatGPT’s recommendations. The core product needs to“maintain objectivity,” said Shomair, claiming a business “should not have an impact on how frequently it appears.”

That doesn’t mean the information is irrelevant to visibility though. Shomair said it could help ChatGPT surface a business in more relevant conversations since the platform would have a clearer picture of what that business offers.

Apple is placing strict limits on its staffers’ AI token consumption. Should the marketing sector take a similar path?

Q4 credits may bring advertisers to Reddit, but concerns about measurement and its ad capabilities make it harder to secure year-round spend.

The Trade Desk’s Jeff Green hints at potential M&A, while rival DSP Viant buys Waymark.

Get access to tools and analysis to stay ahead of the trends transforming media and marketing

#7188. The Brand Isn’t Always the Star ft. Alyssa Schaefer of MDVIP

Most CMOs are trained to make the brand the loudest thing on the block.

But what if the brand isn’t supposed to be the star?

In this episode of Marketing Vanguard, Jenny Rooney sits down with MDVIP chief marketing officer Alyssa Schaefer to unpack what marketing looks like when trust sits in the relationship between a doctor and a patient.

She breaks down the strategy that built 33% unaided awareness from an imperfect starting point at Laurel Road, why MDVIP’s doctors and patients are unusually aligned, and how she balances hyperlocal physician marketing with broader brand investment.

Alyssa Schaefer is CMO at MDVIP. Previously, she served as the first CMO of Laurel Road, where she helped rebrand the company for a national audience and built strong awareness among physicians. Her career spans American Express, fintech and healthcare, with experience across brand, product, partnerships, direct marketing and general management.

At MDVIP, she now leads marketing across two connected audiences—doctors and patients. Her focus is on growing the business without losing sight of the human relationship at the center of the model.

[07:00] A Brand Is What You Make of It — When Alyssa joined Laurel Road, the brand name had already been chosen and the customer research she expected did not exist. Rather than restart everything, she built around what she had. Within a couple of years, Laurel Road reached 33% unaided awareness among doctors in its target segment. The lesson for marketers is that imperfect starting conditions do not excuse weak execution.

[10:38] What Happens When Everyone Actually Wants the Same Outcome? — Alyssa points to something she rarely encountered in banking: MDVIP’s doctors, patients and business are largely pulling in the same direction. Doctors want more time to provide better care, patients want more time and attention and the company benefits when both happen. That alignment gives marketing a much stronger story than trying to manufacture differentiation on top of conflicting incentives.

[17:06] How Do You Ride a Health Trend Without Chasing One? — From VO2 max to perimenopause, health conversations are moving quickly. Alyssa’s approach is to pay attention without letting marketing hype drive the product. MDVIP’s medical leadership helps determine which signals deserve a place in the offering, while marketing adapts the language around genuine needs already emerging in culture.

[22:22] Why MDVIP Doesn’t Want to Be the Star of Its Own Marketing — MDVIP needs national awareness, but the actual trust sits with individual physicians. Alyssa balances the two through hyperlocal PR, community events and patient forums alongside SEO, SEM and broader brand activity. Her rule is clear and relevant to marketers – build the brand without overshadowing the people customers are actually choosing.

Jenny Rooney is Chief Brand and Community Officer, leading strategy for the overall ADWEEK brand as well as the ways in which we serve and support our audiences with high-value content, products, partnerships and experiences, notably through our community programs such as Marketing Vanguard.

#7173. Senior ML Engineer - Malware Detection (remote work) at CloudLinux

CloudLinux is a global remote-first company. We are driven by our principles: do the right thing, employees first, we are remote first, and we deliver high-volume, low-cost Linux infrastructure and security products that help companies to increase the efficiency of their operations. Every person on our team supports each other and does what we can to ensure we all are successful.

Check out our website for more information: https://cloudlinux.com/

Imunify360 is CloudLinux's security suite for Linux web servers: real-time and background malware scanning, automated cleanup, a WAF, runtime PHP protection (Proactive Defense), a database malware scanner, and IP reputation. It runs on hosting providers' shared, VPS, and dedicated servers, so new web-malware campaigns land on our fleet early.

We sit on one of the largest labeled corpora of live web-server malware in the industry: files, verdicts, cleanup outcomes, and analyst decisions collected from production hosting servers every day. It already drives our signatures, heuristics, and ML models, and there is far more to get out of it. We are looking for a Senior ML Engineer who will turn that data into detection that ships — classifiers, LLM-assisted triage and generated signatures — and keep exploring new ML/LLM methods against it, measured by the product's false-negative and false-positive rates, not by notebook accuracy.

Our dataset focuses on web malware — PHP, JavaScript, HTML phishing kits, infected WordPress and Joomla plugins and themes, database injections — with the context around each file.

The goal is 10x more malware detected at the same or lower false-positive rate. Hand-written rules and faster pipelines will not get there; models trained on our data might.

You get an experimentation track, GPUs, and a direct line to leadership; in return, you bring numbers, not opinions.

You own the path from raw samples to a model in production, and the numbers that prove it helped.

#7170. Senior ML Engineer - Email Security (remote work) at CloudLinux

CloudLinux is a global remote-first company. We are driven by our principles: do the right thing, employees first, we are remote first, and we deliver high-volume, low-cost Linux infrastructure and security products that help companies to increase the efficiency of their operations. CloudLinux Inc. develops the Imunify family of security products, designed to protect hosting environments from a wide range of cyber threats. One of these solutions is Imunify Email, an email security product that protects hosting servers against spam, phishing, and other email-based threats.

Imunify Email filters mail on about 5,000 hosting servers. Today the filter is rspamd with rules and a scoring model we train in-house. We are replacing it with a transformer we trained ourselves that runs on the customer's own CPU in under 100 MB and already beats rspamd at every operating point we measured. We are looking for a Senior Machine Learning Engineer to join our team and work alongside the engineer currently responsible for both the existing spam-filtering pipeline and the new ML model. Together, you will drive the development, improvement, and production rollout of our email security technology.

#7169. Beyond the Cookie: How AI Is Transforming Marketing Performance

AI is giving marketers new ways to understand consumers, optimize creative, and improve campaign performance. But how can brands move beyond experimentation to turn these capabilities into measurable business results?

It starts with better data, smarter targeting, and a willingness to rethink how marketing decisions are made.

In this episode of Adspeak by ADWEEK, host editor in chief Ryan Joe brings together Eric Danetz, president at Cognitiv, and Nicole Vinson, VP of agency ecosystem and growth marketing integration lead at Mars, to explore how AI and deep learning are reshaping modern marketing.

The panel discusses why behavioral data offers a more accurate picture of consumer preferences than traditional panel research, and how clean room technology can bring together different data sources to create a more complete understanding of audiences.

They also explore how a pilot-first approach helps organizations test AI applications before committing to large-scale implementation. Real-world examples illustrate the potential, including reducing creative analytics from 100 hours to under 10 and achieving a 36% lift in campaign results through more precise audience targeting.

The conversation also addresses the organizational challenges of adopting AI, from establishing responsible governance to building partnerships that prioritize long-term business outcomes over technology for its own sake.

Eric Danetz is president of Cognitiv, the deep learning advertising platform helping brands predict consumer behavior and deliver more relevant advertising. An accomplished executive, he has held senior global leadership positions at organizations including Thomson Reuters, AccuWeather, Time Inc., IAC, CBS, and Ziff Davis. With extensive experience scaling businesses, driving revenue growth, leading large teams, and navigating M&A, he is also an active investor, board member, and advisor to numerous organizations across the technology and media industries.

Nicole Vinson is VP of agency ecosystem and growth marketing integration lead at Mars, where she leads the transformation of Mars’ global marketing agency ecosystem. With more than 20 years of experience in digital marketing, media, and strategic marketing management, she previously led Kellanova’s Global Marketing Excellence Center of Excellence and its global digital marketing transformation. Her work focuses on leveraging data, technology, and AI to build more effective marketing strategies, strengthen consumer engagement, and accelerate brand growth.

#7161. The 3 Paths to Being a Better Marketer That Have Nothing to Do with Marketing.

Every CMO knows the drill: as a brand scales, founders and the executive committee reach out asking how to spot true marketing leadership in a sea of impressive resumes.

My answer is always the same: It’s not easy, but it is simple. Finding the right person requires looking beyond pedigree, predictable career steps, and whether they have done the exact job before. When I was hired by my CEO, he said 10 words I will never forget: “It’s not about what you’ve done, but who you are.” That sentiment still holds true today.

Only 6% of CXOs think the best profile for effective CMOs is a strong academic foundation. In other words, 94% of leaders who have a CMO reporting to them don’t think this is the best profile. But exclusive research conducted by NewtonX and ADWEEK for the Marketing Vanguard program reveals there is no single path to CMO effectiveness. Instead, it suggests the experiences gained outside of marketing are the secret to success.

Whether you’re formally educated or not, becoming a great top marketer is about the ability to gain and apply cross-functional experience. For example, it isn’t surprising that the top profile (24%) for a CMO leader is the “Hybrid Builder,” someone who combines academic marketing credentials with experience in product, sales, or consulting.  The second most popular profile (20%) is ‘no clear pattern,’ indicating effective CMOs can come from diverse backgrounds.

So what then are the diverse backgrounds that interest leadership? Commercial Operations, Entrepreneurship, and Data Science/Tech all rose to the top.

Those with Operations backgrounds represent 18% of responses. They have experience owning revenue, P&L, or general management. “It serves as evidence that they can connect marketing decisions to commercial performance and enterprise level outcomes,” says Jasmin Siegle, Senior Director of Strategic Insights & Analytics at NewtonX.

Entrepreneurs represent 16% and have experience operating under pressure. Having built or scaled a business signals comfort with ambiguity, resource constraints and accountability. All these are qualities that can help CMOs move beyond campaign execution and contribute more broadly to business strategy.

And in an increasingly measured and mar tech driven world, those with a data-science background are considered attractive cross-overs. Digitally native leaders also represented 16%. “Their value is not simply technical expertise; it is the ability to use data, technology, and analytics to guide decisions and demonstrate marketing’s contribution to measurable business outcomes,” says Jasmin Siegle, Senior Director of Strategic Insights & Analytics at NewtonX.

Taking a step back and looking at the similarities across disciplines, a picture emerges that suggests marketing effectiveness is defined more by behaviors and skills, not a single background or education.

For those that have it, appreciate your marketing education but don’t be complacent. The role is being rewritten in real time. There will always be something to learn. If your experience lies in a different field but you’ve always been interested in marketing, your skills are more transferable than you think.

Those who are best positioned for success are the ones who have the marketing background and education, and diverse cross-functional experience.

The research tells us that above all, everyone is on their own journey. Success rewards those who are proactive in benchmarking themselves and then creating steps-to-the-goal so they can possess the necessary skills for modern marketing leadership.

Jackie Cutrone is the Principal and Founder of Lightning Point Group. With nearly two decades in brand strategy and marketing, Jackie is the former CMO and current Chief Brand Advisor of NewtonX, where she architected the brand and demand engine from the ground up, driving 5x growth and effectively positioning NewtonX as the leader in AI-powered B2B research. Prior to NewtonX she led campaigns at Arnold, Havas and Interbrand, where she partnered with Fortune 500 brands such as American Express, Deloitte, AT&T, BNY, Gartner, Bain, LinkedIn, HP and dozens more. A recognized thought leader, she has authored industry-shaping content, led executive workshops, and spoken at Cannes Lions, WPP, IIEX North America, and ADWEEK events. Jackie holds degrees from Columbia University and the S.I. Newhouse School of Public Communications at Syracuse University. She has taught graduate classes at Columbia University and Pratt Institute.

#7144. Omnicom Makes Tom Schwenger CEO of Omnicom Health Marketing

Omnicom appointed Tom Schwenger as chief executive officer of Omnicom Health’s marketing branch, the company announced last week.

Schwenger will manage Omnicom Health’s global marketing agencies. This role will entail developing and implementing client engagement models that cater to clients across the pharma, biotech, and consumer healthcare industries. The team, including Schwenger and the group’s agency leaders and integrated client leads, will continue to leverage Omni and Acxiom, Omnicom’s life science data and AI marketing intelligence platforms.

Schwenger will work alongside Michael Larson, chair of Omnicom Health, while transitioning into the role.

“I’m excited to join Omnicom Health at such a pivotal moment, leveraging the scale and capabilities of the newly combined organization to help clients navigate the rapid transformation across the pharma, biotech, and healthcare sectors,” said Schwenger. “This internal evolution, coupled with powerful market dynamics, creates an extraordinary opportunity to drive innovation and growth.”

Schwenger will be taking over the role from Dana Maiman, who served as Omnicom Health’s CEO for the past year. He brings more than three decades of experience driving growth and leading strategic initiatives in pharma, biotech, and healthcare. Schwenger was previously president and chief customer officer at Veeva Systems, a cloud computing company for the life sciences industry, where he encouraged early AI adoption and helped triple the organization’s revenue.

Omnicom Health’s appointment of Schwenger follows the organization’s acquisition of Interpublic Group (IPG), which formed the world’s largest advertising holding company. #7143. Container Engineer at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth. More than 9 million developers and 250,000+ customers now build on Supabase, and the majority of new databases on the platform are created by AI app builders — so the systems underneath have to stay reliable while they grow fast.

Container Engineering (ConEng) owns the internal container platform that runs Supabase's multi-tenant, multi-region services — the managed Kubernetes platform that other engineering teams build and deploy on top of. We own cluster lifecycle and cluster classes, multi-cluster and multi-region topology, capacity, scheduling, isolation and runtime safety, the higher-level platform APIs, the Kubernetes deployment contract and Argo semantics, rollout primitives, and runtime configuration and secret injection.

Your job is to make the platform itself trustworthy, observable, and cheap to operate at scale — and to raise the reliability bar for everyone who depends on it. This is not a gatekeeper role. You'll make the reliable path the easy path: building the automation and platform APIs that let product teams run safely by default, instrumenting what we operate, and making sure that when something breaks we detect it and recover fast.

Tooling & ecosystem — deep knowledge of the container and orchestration landscape (Kubernetes, containerd/OCI, the CNCF ecosystem) and a clear point of view on how to compose it.

Software engineering — designing and building the platform's automation, controllers, and internal tooling as production code, including solutions that don't come pre-shaped by an existing tool.

Operational / SRE practice — the discipline (SLOs, error budgets, incident response, ORRs) that runs the platform at scale as a predictable one.

We expect you to be genuinely deep in at least one of these, and many of our strongest candidates are deep in two (usually leaning toward one). Bringing real strength across all three is rare and exceptional — and even then, no one is equally strong in all three at once, so you'd focus where you're most needed at any given time. Different profiles are welcome here; tell us which of these is your center of gravity.

Regardless of the skill set, all team members carry a share of the team's on-call rota, responding to platform incidents and driving them to resolution and follow-up.

Define SLIs/SLOs and error budgets for the container platform itself, and build error-budget policies that actually gate platform changes.

Own the Operational Readiness Review (ORR) process for new platform capabilities and major changes.

Design and track metrics for platform uptime, and increase observability into the platform by capturing the right metrics, logs, and traces across the platform layers.

Drive the incident-to-improvement pipeline: facilitate blameless postmortems, connect findings to readiness gaps, spot repeat failure patterns, and push through systemic fixes.

Build and maintain the platform's automation, controllers, operators, and internal tooling — writing production code and shipping it, not just wiring together existing tools.

Design and implement the platform-side CI/CD and rollout primitives that speed up platform deployments while working within proper change and release management.

Implement and maintain security automation for the platform: intrusion detection, automated remediation, and patch management.

Identify and quantify operational toil, and build the automation that removes it rather than just documenting it.

Contribute to the platform's continual testing automation — the test suites and pipelines that validate platform releases are fit for purpose before they reach tenants.

Act as a platform reliability and architecture expert — contribute to failure-mode analysis, dependency mapping, and resilience design for multi-tenant container workloads.

Evaluate and evolve the platform's tooling ecosystem (orchestration, runtime, networking, isolation, and observability stack) with a clear, opinionated view on trade-offs.

Improve system architecture to reduce infrastructure cost while balancing security, reliability, and performance across tenants.

Guide teams building on the platform to design sustainable on-call practices for their own services: alert quality, escalation paths, runbook coverage, and noise reduction.

Have 5+ years in platform engineering, container/Kubernetes platform operations, SRE, or production infrastructure, and have operated production systems at scale and carried on-call for them.

Are genuinely deep in at least one — ideally two — of the three strength areas below (and tell us which is your center of gravity):

Tooling & ecosystem — know the container/orchestration landscape well: Kubernetes, containerd and the OCI/CRI standards, and the surrounding CNCF ecosystem (e.g. Argo CD/Argo Rollouts for GitOps and progressive delivery; Cilium/eBPF networking and the Gateway API; Karpenter/KEDA-style autoscaling; and workload-isolation runtimes such as gVisor or Kata Containers). You reason about trade-offs rather than reaching for defaults.

Software engineering — write real production code (Go and/or a comparable systems language) and can build controllers, operators, and platform APIs — stepping back to the underlying problem and building the right solution when no off-the-shelf tool fits.

Operational / SRE practice — are fluent in SLOs, error budgets, and operational KPIs; have led incident response and blameless postmortems; and have driven down MTTD/MTTR for systems you operated.

Have worked with large-scale multi-tenant systems and understand isolation as a spectrum — from namespaces, RBAC, network policies, and resource quotas through to stronger primitives such as virtual clusters (e.g. vCluster) or VM-class runtimes.

Operate confidently on AWS (multiple accounts, IAM, VPC) and are comfortable with infrastructure-as-code (Pulumi preferred; Terraform/CDK also fine) and Kubernetes.

Script and automate to eliminate toil rather than absorb it.

Communicate clearly with both infrastructure specialists and product engineers, and thrive in async, globally distributed teams.

Are comfortable navigating ambiguity and iterating toward better systems over time.

Experience building and operating a multi-tenant Kubernetes platform that internal teams deploy onto.

Familiarity with policy engines (Kyverno, or OPA/Gatekeeper) and cost-attribution tooling (OpenCost/Kubecost) for per-tenant chargeback/showback.

Experience with managed database platforms or Postgres.

Familiarity with OpenTelemetry, Prometheus/VictoriaMetrics, Grafana, or similar observability tooling.

#7136. Skydance to Embrace Bundling Across Business Functions

$73 million – Paramount Skydance’s third-quarter net earnings before it acquired Warner Bros. Discovery in October.

$7 billion – Paramount Skydance’s revenue, a 5% year-over-year growth, before its acquisition was completed.

$6 billion – How much Skydance expects to achieve in run-rate synergies over the next three years.

20% – Rise in sports advertising commitments year-over-year for current and upcoming sports seasons leading into 2027.

Over the next few months and possibly years, bundling will take place at Skydance. After completing its acquisition of Warner Bros. Discovery in October, the combined conglomerate is now looking to slim down and cut its roughly $80 billion debt.

In corporate speak, Skydance is looking to consolidate a lot of back-end and front-end operations. In regular parlance, this is just bundling, and the media company is looking to bundle much of what it has now to achieve these savings. It has started more than 20 of what it calls “workstreams” to identify areas of possible cuts.

The Skydance third-quarter earnings letter to shareholders said the company aims to reduce costs at least $2.2 billion by “bundling” redundant back-office and corporate functions. It aims to “bundle” its media planning and buying across business units to eliminate audience overlap, coordinate campaigns across those units and maximize media efficiency at scale.

Other areas will also see bundling, including technology. Skydance will unify its enterprise tech stack, streaming ecosystem and broadcast architecture, merging parallel technology functions. It will also bundle procurement and vendor management operations, saying its combined purchasing levels will provide economies of scale by consolidating purchasing decisions into one centralized group.

“This is a growth story; we are shifting from legacy media to building a next-generation media and entertainment company powered by creativity and technology,” said Ynon Kreiz, Skydance’s co-chief executive officer.

Mark Mwachiro is a TVNewser contributor who has been writing for the blog since March 2022. Based out of New York, Mark has also contributed to ADWEEK.

#7133. Chief Customer Officer at Xapo Bank

Diversity is at the heart of who we are at Xapo Bank. We’re a fully distributed team of over 160 Xapiens that work remotely from 50+ countries around the world.

Our beginning: A world that enjoys economic freedom and wealth protection,no matter where you live or who is running your country.To achieve that, we search the world for the best people for the job. We work hard, think globally, and inspire each other to learn and grow. We are committed to changing the way things are done.

Although we are headquartered in Gibraltar, this is a full time, 100% remote position Work from anywhere!

Xapo's product suite has grown from a Bitcoin vault into a full member proposition: BTC and USD accounts that pay interest, a global card with Bitcoin cashback, Bitcoin-backed loans, a growing suite of BTC wealth and credit products, and access to stocks, ETFs, and stablecoins, all inside one regulated relationship.

Xapo is a bank, and a bank earns on the money its members choose to hold with it. Each of those products shipped well. What hasn't yet existed is a single owner of the economics of the customer relationship: one person accountable for winning the right members, growing what they hold with us, and keeping them, and for the balance-sheet products that make that possible.

The Chief Customer Officer is a new role at the top of the house, reporting to the CEO. You will run the stream that grows what members hold with Xapo, and you will own the Customer P&L, retail and institutional. It brings growth, client coverage, product management, and product design under one leader who is judged on customer contribution, the assets attracted and kept, and the members who stay. This is a customer-focused role in the most commercial sense: what a member earns from us, not the feature we shipped, is the centre of every decision.

The measure of success is a Customer P&L that grows on what members hold and keep: LTV:CAC improving, approved-to-paid rising, churn falling, and customer contribution the business can plan against.

This is a banking role, not a requirements-gathering role, a marketing role, or a role you can run from a dashboard. Products are priced on the balance sheet and judged on the money they attract and keep.

This part matters as much as the mandate. The known failure mode for this role, in any company, is a CCO with a polished customer-insight deck and no ownership of the number. That is disqualifying here.

Day 30: The Customer P&L baseline is documented across retail and institutional: customer contribution, LTV:CAC, approved-to-paid, churn, net paid-member growth, and assets attracted and kept. You have formed your own independent view of where members are won, lost, and under-served, and shared it with the CEO and Board.Day 60: The unified Customer operating model is live: one set of key results across Growth, Client Coverage, Mission PMs, and Product Design, read weekly from one source. Every mission PM owns a product's contribution and its price, and the roadmap is ranked on what it earns, built with Finance and the line leads.Day 90: At least one measurable improvement in LTV:CAC, approved-to-paid, churn, or net new AUM flow is live and attributable to a change you made, not a feature ship, an outcome moved. Every cell in your line has defined key results and an owner who explains the number every week.

Shape the FutureImprove lives through cutting-edge technology, work 100% remotely from anywhere in the world.

Great work-life balanceBuild amazing things with a balance of autonomy and collaborative teamwork. Set your own work schedule and make use of a flexible PTO plan when you need to recharge.

Expect ExcellenceCollaborate, learn, and grow with a high-performance team. Learn how you learn best - from books to conferences, you’ll get a yearly budget for your individual learning and development goals.

#7119. X Previews Labels That Tell Users Why Its AI System Flagged Their Posts

X’s AI-powered content moderation system once mistakenly labeled a post featuring an image of a SpaceX rocket launch as “adult content.” No one, including the author of the post, could see why.

That’s the problem Michael O’Herlihy, a Twitter veteran who recently rejoined X as head of safety, wants to fix.

In the future, X’s content moderation systems may show their decisioning process, he suggested at the Brand & AI Safety Summit in New York on Tuesday (Nov. 10). There, O’Herlihy previewed designs for labels displayed beneath content on X that explain what its AI detected in a post, detail how exactly the post’s reach was limited after being flagged, and allow users to appeal the decision.

#7108. Customer Growth Manager at Uscreen

Uscreen is the video monetization platform for businesses building recurring revenue from video content. Our customers replace a patchwork stack of separate hosting, community, courses, email, and app vendors with one branded experience across web, mobile, and TV.

The platform is only half of it. We believe growth requires behavior, not just software. Customers don't stall because a feature is missing. They stall because they priced wrong, never built an email list, or launched an offer nobody wanted. Coaching is how we fix that, and it's part of the product rather than an add-on.

You own a segment, and you run it like a growth marketer.

We have a cohort of smaller video businesses where retention is not where it should be. Nobody owns it today. You will. The revenue that stays in that segment, and the revenue that grows inside it, is yours.

This is a coaching role for someone with a growth marketer's mindset. We wouldn't hire a demand gen marketer who couldn't analyze the state of the business and figure out what's needed, and we won't hire a coach who can't either. You'll look at a customer's business the way an owner would: where the revenue comes from, where it leaks, and what to do next. You pull your own numbers to get there. When a customer needs a custom report to answer a growth question, you build it yourself.

It's a one-to-many job. Your cohort is larger than a 1:1 book, so the work is designing coaching that scales: cohort programs, group sessions, office hours, and triggered outreach when the data says a business is drifting. You'll build that motion rather than inherit it.

Success is measured in dollars: gross revenue retention in your cohort first, expansion second. Not tickets closed, not calls booked.

A coach with a growth marketer's mindset. Maybe you've run growth or demand gen for a small business, built your own audience, or coached founders through their numbers. You understand how a membership business makes money, you pull your own analytics to see what's working, and you're comfortable doing it in front of a client. What matters is that you can find the real constraint in a business, then sit with the operator and say the honest thing.

That’s why we want to empower people at scale by leveraging cutting edge technologies like AI, and crafting user experiences with outstanding aesthetics.

- Human Intelligence (coaches, peers, and mentors)

- Structured Intelligence (protocols, mental models, rituals),

- Technological Intelligence (AI personalization, feedback loops, adaptive journeys)

To create one seamless ecosystem: Paradox OS.

This is why we are looking for a PM to make Paradox a category-defining global brand loved for its product.

This is a full-time position from our beautiful offices in Dubai, Paris or Lisbon or remotely anywhere in the world.

We believe self-help is broken.Most learning platforms are used the same way : consuming generic theories, getting quick highs, ending with no real change.

At Paradox, we believe people deserve more.More than content. More than motivation.

They deserve a system that knows them, one that adapts, challenges, and grows with them.

We believe that every human learns differently, and that technology can do more than entertain or save time.It can translate insight into transformation, through AI personalization, lean experimentation, and deep psychological understanding.

Because learning shouldn’t just make you feel inspired for a day, it should change how you live, think, and create.

At Paradox, we want to re-invent how people grow.

We do not build another course or coaching app but the Operating System for Greatness.

Founded by David Laroche - a creator followed by over 2 million people and known for his interviews with icons like Tony Parker, Mike Horn, Richard Branson, and Gad Elmaleh, Paradox aims to build something that will live beyond any individual (including its founder), for decades to come.

Our ambition is to compete with players like Mindvalley (150M ARR), but with a sharper vision, grounded protocols for achievers, people who want more for their life, refusing mediocrity.

To do that, we bring together designers, tech, coaches, and filmmakers to create learning experiences that are cinematic, precise, and effective.

We move fast, aim high, and hold ourselves to the standards of Apple, Spotify, and MasterClass. No fluff, focused on meaningful impact.

While others hide their numbers, we want to let our results speak loudly for us.

Get obsessed with transforming our clients’ lives and let those transformations be our marketing.

We want to build an AI-native company, with agents in every department doing the work alongside our people.

In product, that means building a self-learning AI for product management: a system that learns from every user signal, experiment and release, and gets sharper at deciding what to build next.

For our clients, it means a self-learning AI that serves them through psychology and coaching, and gets better at helping each person change with every interaction.

You won’t just use AI tools: you’ll help design the agents, loops and feedback systems that make Paradox learn faster than anyone else.

-----------------------------------------

As Product Manager, you’ll be the architect of a new category, transforming growth into a living ecosystem powered by AI, behavioral science, and design.

You won’t just build features, you’ll craft experiences that rewire behavior.

You’re the architect bringing clarity between vision and execution, the person who turns “we should build this” into “it’s live, here’s the data and tangible impact.”

Protocols : guided frameworks grounded in psychology that rewire perception, emotion, and action.

Pods : human connection, accountability, and shared rituals.

Loops : adaptive, AI-powered coaching cycles that evolve with each user.

Help marketing leverage this data to fuel authentic, evidence-based storytelling — showing the world what real transformation looks like.

#7083. Kyndryl: VRO Lead

At Kyndryl, we design, build, manage and modernize the mission-critical technology systems that the world depends on every day. So why work at Kyndryl? We are always moving forward – always pushing ourselves to go further in our efforts to build a more equitable, inclusive world for our employees, our customers and our communities.

In the Value Realization Office at Kyndryl, you will play a critical role in ensuring that our customers achieve and maximize the tangible business outcomes promised in their digital transformation journeys. You will act as the strategic driver behind establishing, measuring, and protecting value across complex IT outsourcing, enterprise resource planning (ERP), and large-scale infrastructure engagements post-contract signature.

In this role, you will define and execute the VRO operating model from the ground up, establishing governance cadences, RACI frameworks, and executive escalation paths tailored to each engagement. You will partner closely with client finance and operations leaders to rigorously construct and validate operational baselines, translating overarching deal cases into transparent, actionable benefits registers equipped with defined KPIs, milestones, and auditable proof points.

Your financial acumen and analytical rigor will be central to defining robust measurement models for hard cost savings, risk and cost avoidance, productivity uplift, and service quality enhancements. By designing automated scorecards and dashboards, tracking realized gains, and diagnosing root causes behind value leakage or process drift, you will keep critical customer engagements on course. Furthermore, you will lead high-impact value reviews with C-suite stakeholders and Kyndryl account executives, turning proven realized value into a catalyst for account renewals, expansion, and long-term client advocacy.

Kyndryl’s mission to modernize enterprise IT and drive business transformation means that within the Value Realization Office, you will be positioned at the vital intersection of technology, finance, and client strategy. You will lead high-profile transformation initiatives, mentor and direct VRO analysts across concurrent engagements, and actively contribute to building reusable assets, benchmark repositories, and measurement standards that scale across our global practice.

Kyndryl currently does not require employees to be fully vaccinated against COVID-19, however, if you are hired to work at a client, customer, or partner location, you may be required to show proof of vaccination to align with their respective COVID-19 vaccination policies.  Those who believe they are eligible may apply for a medical or religious accommodation prior to the start of employment.

You are good at what you do and possess the proven consulting and financial depth to prove it. You bring a growth mindset, navigating complex transformation headwinds with clarity and decisive problem-solving. You are relentlessly customer-focused—committed to ensuring our clients see verifiable return on investment and financial transparency. Finally, you are empathetic, collaborative, and borderless, partnering seamlessly across organizational change management (OCM), delivery, and executive teams to build trust and achieve shared success.

The base compensation range for the position in the U.S. is -

$143,640 to $273,000 based on a full-time schedule reflects the minimum and maximum target compensation Kyndryl, in good faith, believes it may pay for the role at the time of this posting.

Your actual compensation may vary depending on multiple factors legally permitted in the applicable jurisdiction, including, but not limited to, geography, relevant job-related skills, education and experience, certification and licenses, internal pay equity, and market and business consideration. The pay or salary range will not be below any applicable state, city or local minimum wage requirement.

There is a different applicable compensation range for the following work locations:

California (San Francisco Bay Area): $172,440 to $327,600

California (All Other): $158,040 to $300,360

Massachusetts/ Virginia: $143,640 to $300,360

This position will be eligible for Kyndryl’s discretionary annual bonus program, based on performance and subject to the terms of Kyndryl’s applicable plans. You may also receive a comprehensive benefits package which includes medical and dental coverage, disability, retirement benefits, paid leave, and paid time off. Note: If this is a sales commission eligible role, you will be eligible to participate in a sales commission plan in lieu of the annual discretionary bonus program.

Applications will be accepted on a rolling basis.

Diversity is a whole lot more than what we look like or where we come from, it’s how we think and who we are. We welcome people of all cultures, backgrounds, and experiences. But we’re not doing it single-handily: Our Kyndryl Inclusion Networks are only one of many ways we create a workplace where all Kyndryls can find and provide support and advice. This dedication to welcoming everyone into our company means that Kyndryl gives you – and everyone next to you – the ability to bring your whole self to work, individually and collectively, and support the activation of our equitable culture. That’s the Kyndryl Way.

With state-of-the-art resources and Fortune 100 clients, every day is an opportunity to innovate, build new capabilities, new relationships, new processes, and new value. Kyndryl cares about your well-being and prides itself on offering benefits that give you choice, reflect the diversity of our employees and support you and your family through the moments that matter – wherever you are in your life journey. Our employee learning programs give you access to the best learning in the industry to receive certifications, including Microsoft, Google, Amazon, Skillsoft, and many more. Through our company-wide volunteering and giving platform, you can donate, start fundraisers, volunteer, and search over 2 million non-profit organizations.  At Kyndryl, we invest heavily in you, we want you to succeed so that together, we will all succeed.

#7079. Senior Product Builder - Hub at Camunda

Camunda is the enterprise platform for agentic orchestration, enabling organizations to coordinate AI agents, people, and systems across complex, end-to-end business processes. With built-in governance, auditability, and human oversight, Camunda gives enterprises the control they need to move AI from pilots to production — safely and at scale. Trusted by over 700 organizations worldwide, including 9 of top 10 US banks, Camunda helps enterprises boost operational efficiency, accelerate time-to-value, and deliver better customer experiences.Fully remote and global, we are in the middle of something bigger: transforming into an AI-first organisation, built on our own platform. We use Agentic AI to automate, orchestrate intelligent processes, and elevate human contribution across every team.Named GP Bullhound’s Top 100 Next Unicorn list, 2025 Great Place to Work certified. Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

Camunda Hub is where enterprises run Camunda at scale. From Hub they manage their clusters and tenants, decide who can do what, and roll changes out across the whole organization. We’re looking for a Senior Product Builder to own the backend and architecture that let Hub manage a whole fleet of clusters. Your first focus will be the mechanism that sends access, secret, tenant and licence changes out to every connected cluster and brings deployments, health and audit records back. From there, you’ll shape Hub’s foundations: its event model, the service boundaries and data model other teams build on, and how Hub behaves when things fail or get upgraded. The platform is your product. Your users are platform administrators, Centres of Excellence and the builders across Camunda who depend on what you create. You’ll join a new two-person pod in our remote-first team. You’ll have real ownership as the person who makes the call, and you’ll build AI-first, from written specs through to production. Our customers run mission-critical processes on Camunda, so your work will matter to them every day.

Take over Hub's fleet propagation mechanism from the team building its first version, and make it work for access, secrets, tenants, licences and custom roles

Write short design documents for foundational changes, get them reviewed by the teams that depend on them, and turn them into tested, shipped code

Design how Hub behaves when things go wrong: offline clusters, partial failures, Hub outages, and clusters being adopted or detached

Build the path from clusters back to Hub, starting with deployment inventory, health, audit records and licence status

Design Hub's first event and webhook mechanism, so admins and agents can react to changes instead of polling for them

Join the pod's rotations and Hub on-call, and help other builders take on backend work through reviews, guidelines and contract tests

Experience building and running backend systems in production that other teams or customers depend on

Comfort taking a problem nobody owns yet and turning it into a design, a plan and shipped code

Product judgment for technical users: you talk to the people who use your APIs and make trade-offs on their behalf

Clear, short writing that lets a remote team review and decide asynchronously

Care for how a system behaves when parts of it fail, not only for the happy path

Designed and shipped a control plane or similar system that pushes desired state to many targets. Knows outbox or event-log patterns, idempotent apply, versioning, retries and drift reconciliation

Senior backend engineering in Java (API design, relational data models, multi-tenant SaaS services) and hands-on Helm and Kubernetes for self-managed installations

Software architecture and failure analysis: partial failure, blast radius, consistency trade-offs. Has debugged production incidents in a distributed system and is ready for on-call

Has shaped an API or platform as a product, and writes design docs other teams can build on. Gets agreement across teams without formal authority

Builds AI-first from written specifications and checks AI output as carefully as any other code

Kubernetes operators or GitOps tools, for example Argo CD

OIDC, SCIM and permission models, or secret managers and licence systems

Camunda 8 or another workflow or orchestration engine

Enough frontend skill to build a thin UI on top of a backend capability

We offer competitive, fair, and transparent compensation. Salary ranges are location-based, with Standard and Major markets (global tech hubs) reflecting local competition.

The Annual Total Target Cash (base salary + 100% variable target, where applicable) shown below spans from the minimum in a Standard market to the maximum in a Major market. Final offers depend on skills, experience, and location, and we typically hire in the first half of the range to allow room for growth:

United States: $143,800.00 to $231,900.00

United Kingdom: £90,300.00 to £148,500.00

If you’re based elsewhere, you’ll be hired via Remote.com (our global employer partner), and your Talent Acquisition Partner will provide a personalized Total Rewards Calculator after your first interview.

Equity: We also offer equity (where applicable) through our Virtual Stock Option Plan (VSOP).

We invest in your wellbeing, growth, and ability to connect, along with perks that support you no matter where you’re based. Our benefits are globally designed and locally delivered where applicable.

Remote & Flexible: Work from anywhere with the setup that suits you, home office budget, co-working space support, and flexible time off to recharge when you need it.

In Person Connection: We invest in meaningful face time through our Annual Kickoff (Vienna in 2025, Madrid in 2026!), team offsites, and Camundi Connection Budgets, including contributing to meetups while travelling,, and local gatherings with fellow Camundi.

#7068. Software Engineer - AI Tooling Infrastructure at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We’re looking for a Software Engineer, AI Tooling Infrastructure to join our AI Tooling team and help build and run the backend services behind Supabase’s AI products. The Supabase MCP server is one example of what the team runs in production, alongside the services that support agent skills and the dashboard assistant. You’ll work closely with the Platform, SRE, Auth, Billing and Edge Functions teams, and with the engineers on the AI Tooling team who build the product surfaces.

The AI Tooling team runs its own services in production and carries its own on-call. Traffic from AI clients and agents behaves differently from traffic from people: hundreds of calls a minute, retry loops, and no reading of deprecation warnings. This role makes sure the services they use are reliable, correctly authorized and observable. It is a systems and infrastructure role rather than a product AI role, and it is not an SRE role. You will build and operate these systems, not only advise on them.

This role is ideal for someone who thrives in async, fast-paced environments, owns what they ship in production, and is excited about building developer tools that scale to millions.

Build and operate the production backend services behind Supabase’s AI products, including the MCP server: deployment, scaling, configuration and release.

Take ownership of how those services are structured, including splitting or reshaping a service when that makes it safer to run and easier to change.

Define, measure and meet reliability targets for the services the team runs. Set SLOs with the SRE team, track error budgets, and use them to decide what to fix first.

Participate in the AI Tooling team on-call rotation, and be a first responder for incidents in those services. Investigate, fix, coordinate with other teams during incidents, and write the follow-ups and runbooks.

Work the AI Tooling team support queue alongside on-call, and turn recurring issues into fixes rather than repeated manual answers.

Own authentication and authorization for AI and agent clients: OAuth apps, scoped tokens, and permissions that make sense for agents as well as humans, including how we change or deprecate a permission without breaking existing integrations.

Own observability for the services the team runs: metrics, traces, logs, dashboards and alerts, so we find problems before customers do.

Handle capacity and abuse for agent traffic: rate limits, cost and safety controls, and behavior under load or when a dependency degrades.

Improve the architecture of the services the team owns as they grow, including splitting or reshaping a service when that makes it safer to run and easier to change.

Work with the Platform team on shared code, review ownership and release process, and with the SRE team on infrastructure, alerting and incident practice.

Contribute to the code of the AI Tooling team’s tools, such as the MCP server, where it affects reliability and security, in partnership with the engineers who own the product surface.

Have 5+ years of experience building and running backend services in production, and have carried on-call for them.

Write strong TypeScript or Go, and are comfortable working in the other.

Have designed and operated APIs with real consumers: versioning, error handling, rate limiting, and backwards-compatible change.

Know OAuth, OIDC, JWT and API authorization well enough to debug them in production and to explain where implementations commonly deviate from the spec.

Have a good understanding of PostgreSQL and relational databases.

Are comfortable with CI/CD, Docker and containerized deployments, and with infrastructure as code. Any tool is fine.

Can debug across services: follow a slow or failing request through several systems using metrics, traces and logs.

Communicate clearly in writing, work with a wide range of stakeholders, and thrive in async, globally distributed teams.

Are comfortable navigating ambiguity and moving quickly.

Pick your centre of gravity. We expect you to be deep in at least one of these, and to tell us which:

APIs and auth: You have designed permission models, token scopes or OAuth flows for third-party or machine clients, and you have migrated them without breaking users.

Operations: You have run production services yourself: carried the pager, written alerts and runbooks, used SLOs or similar to decide what to fix, and made shared systems less risky over time.

Gateways and distributed systems: You have built or run proxies or gateways, or debugged hard failures across services (retries, partial failures, upstream outages, fail-open vs fail-closed decisions), and you can reason about load and degradation.

Experience with agent tooling (MCP or similar), or serving traffic from AI agents and LLM clients.

Experience moving a service out of a larger codebase while keeping existing clients working.

Experience with Grafana, OpenTelemetry, Logflare or a similar observability stack, and real SLO work.

Open-source contributions, especially to Node.js, Go, TypeScript or API tooling.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

We move fast, build in public, and use what we ship. If it’s in your project, we probably use it in ours too. We believe deeply in the open-source ecosystem and strive to support—not replace—existing tools and communities.
